I am writing with reference to your reports of ‘unidentified flying objects’, seen on 17" and
29" December 1999. The reports were passed to this office by RAF Leuchars as this office is the
focal point within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to ‘UFOs.’

First it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
‘unidentified flying objects’ it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised foreign military activity.

Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external
military source, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to
identify the precise nature of each sighting reported to us. We believe that rational explanations,
such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources were diverted for
this purpose, but it is not the function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification
service. It would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to do so.

With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
files and can confirm that we received no other reports of ‘UFO’ sightings on either the 17" or 29"
December 1999 from anywhere in Scotland. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating
evidence to suggest that the United Kingdom’s airspace was breached by unauthorised military
aircraft.

2.

I am writing with reference to your report of an ‘unidentified flying object’, seen on 9
December 1999. The report was passed to the Ministry of Defence by Grays Police and this office
is the focal point within the MOD for correspondence relating to ‘UFOs.’

First it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
‘unidentified flying objects’ it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised foreign military activity.

Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external
military source, and to date no "UFO! report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to
identify the precise nature of each sighting reported to us. We believe that rational explanations,
such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources were diverted for
this purpose, but it is not the function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification
service. It would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to do so.

With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
files and can confirm that we received no other reports of ‘UFO’ sightings for 9 December 1999
from anywhere in Essex. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to suggest that
the United Kingdom’s airspace was breached by unauthorised military aircraft.

“UFO' REPORT — 6 Dec 99

Reference: | D/Sec(AS)/64/2 dated 23 Nov 99

1, At Reference, you asked whether the UFO reports in the Tilbury area represented anything
of air defence interest.

2. Having read the attached reports, most observers believed that they were seeing a star. I
have investigated similar lights which flashed red and green in the past; these turned out to be
Venus rising and I have little doubt that a comparable event was in progress on 6 Dec. I do not
intend, therefore to pursue the matter further.

HOW TO PRINT FROM THIS SITE - Firstly, if your computer has the ability to reduce the text aize (some do not) = click ‘View’ on the top toolbar, then
‘Fonts’ from the drop-down menu and finally ‘Smallest’ from the second drop-down menu. Secondly, highlight the text you require to print. Thirdly, DO
NOT use the ‘Print’ icon on lower toolbar - with the text you require to be printed still highlighted, click ‘File’ from the top toolbar and then ‘Print’ frum the
drop-down menu - this will give you the Print Menu. Finally, in the ‘Print range’ of the Print Menu click the ‘Selection’ box followed by “OK" - uhie will
ongure that you only print the text you have selected, and the text will not ‘run off” the right-hand eide of the paper.

HOW TO PRINT FROM THIS SITE - Firstly, if your computer has the ability to reduce the text size (some do not) - click ‘View’ on the top toolbox, then
"Fonts from the drop-down menu and finally ‘Smallest’ from the second drop-down menu. Secondly, highlight tho text you require to print. Thirdly, DO
NOT ure the ‘Print’ icon on lower toolbar - with the text you require lo be printed still highlighted, click ‘File’ from the top toolbar and then ‘Print’ from the
drop-down monu - this will give you the Print Menu. Finally, in the ‘Print range’ of the Print Menu click the ‘Selection’ box followed by ‘OK’ ~ this will
ensure that you only print the text you have selected, and the text will not ‘run off the right-hand ride of the paper.

Subject: UNIDENTIFIED LIGHT IN SKY

At 0900hrs. | received a telephone call from a Mrs.
BEACONSFIELD asking if the MOD Police had had any reports of a large

white circular light in the sky the previous night.

1.

2. On informing her that we had not she explained that at 2320hrs Wednesday 17 November on
letting her dogs out of her house she noticed a large white circular light in the sky in the direction of
MAIDENHEAD. She called her husband and Son in law to see it as well and it then, over a period of
7 to 8 minutes drifted slowly off to the West.

3. | have made enquiries of the Thames Valley Police in the Maidenhead area but they have no
reports of any such light in the sky.

4. The address for GEIR OME is on the A355 between Beaconsfield and Amersham. | have
also informed her that she may be contacted by yourselves.

5. Forwarded for your information and any action you think ne

a

I am writing with reference to your reports of an ‘unidentified flying object’ seen on 25
October 1999, the details of which were passed us RAF Leeming. This office is the focal point
within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to ‘UFOs.’

First it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
‘unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised foreign military activity.

Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external
military source, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to
identify the precise nature of each sighting reported to us. We believe that rational explanations,
such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources were diverted for
this purpose, but it is not the function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification
service. It would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to do so. As a result we
are unable to assist with your particular query.

With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
files and can confirm that we received no other reports of ‘UFO’ sightings for 25 October 1999
from anywhere in Staffordshire. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to suggest
that the United Kingdom’s airspace was breached by unauthorised military aircraft.

Dear SST

I am writing to acknowledge receipt of your report about an unidentified flying object on

25 October 1999. My investigations have revealed that there were no aircraft from RAF Leeming
airborne at the time of the incident, therefore I have forwarded the details you provided to the Ministry of
Defence Air Secretariat which monitors all such reports and decides whether any action should be taken.

Thank you for your concern.

Report of Unidentified Flying Object. , | fm

Details phoned in by a EMIS who lives at Halstead (approx 070
from Stansted range 15 miles). Her daughters were walking across a field
at about 2235 (GMT) when the sighting was made. The telephone call
came to Terminal Control via Stansted ATC.

There were two flights into Stansted, which would match the time; both
small, high winged aircraft. GIL261L, 2225(GMT), a Short360 and
GIL041B 2238(GMT), an ATR72. Both aircraft arrived from the north to
land on Runway 23. The two Stansted Radar Controllers on duty at
Terminal Control also seem to remember Helicopter activity (in the
Halsted area) wearing a Wattisham Radar Label. When ! telephoned
Wattisham however they stated that they had not had any helicopters in
the Halsted area for at least two hours.

A. Iiitte, Time and Duration of sighting C154 July 1999.2335 (local).

C. Exact posit *p+1Xion of Observer. — Sighting was erCeegsection40
two daughters whilst walking across a field in Halsted.

D. How Observed. — Naked eye.

E. Direction in which Object was F_rst Seen. — Not Known.

F. Angular Elevation of Object.~ Not Known.

G. Distance of Object from Observer. - “Close enough for there to
have been some noise if it had been an aircraft”. p600X.

Movements of Object. — see A. H.

Report of Unidentified Flying Object.

Tn accordance with the instructions in the Manual of Air Traffic Services, Section 6
(Reporting Procedures), Chapter 4, para 3 (Unidentified Flying Objects) page 6-22, here are
the details of a report received at this location this evening:

A. Date, time & duration of sighting;
13th April 1999 at 2115 local for 30 - 35 seconds.

B. Description of object;
Very bright point light source flashing white, with 4 quick flashes then a gap, 4 quick
flashes etc. No coloured lights visible, no shape or sound discernible.

C. Exact position of observer;

IS NN East Belfast. Observer was

‘outdoors and stationary.

D. How observed;
Naked eye.
E. Direction in which object first seen;

In direction of, and then apparently following course of old dismantled BCD Railway
in a countrywards (Southeast) direction.

F. Angular elevation of object;
35 - 40 degrees above horizon. Just above level of roof.

G. Distance of object from observer;
Impossible to assess.

National Air Traffic Services Ltd. Registered in England 3155567 - Registered Office: 45-59 Kingsway, London WC2B 6TE.
The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/2008/1

H. Movement of object;
Very rapid - covered distance a commercial aircraft would take 2 minutes to cover, in
30 seconds. Object seemed to climb very rapidly.

J. Meteorological conditions at time;
Clear, but some broken high cloud (object observed to enter cloud, and then
illuminate it from within).

K. Nearby objects;
None relevant (from list of possibles that could be).

L. To whom reported;
Air Traffic Control, Belfast International Airport.
Strandtown RUC station.

M. Name and address of informant;

BELFAST

N. Background info volunteered;
Nil.

O. Other witnesses;
Nil.

P. Date/time of receipt of report;
Tuesday 13th April 1999, 2225 local (BST).

The description of the group flashing strobe type light, along with its period of cycle is very
similar to anti collision HISLs fitted to locally based BN2T type aircraft, Section anne

by the RUC Air Support Unit. From flight progress strip records, it had been airborne to the
South of the Belfast Control Zone just prior to this watch taking over duty. It has not been
possible to ascertain the aircraft’s precise area of operations prior to its recovery to landing at
Aldergrove. The assertion of the witness that the object climbed rapidly and seemed much
faster than commercial aircraft is difficult to correlate to the performance characteristics of
such an aircraft, however.

REPORT OF AN UNIDENTINIED FLYING ORIECT
A Date, time and duration of sighting.
13 March 1999
9.25pm.
Approx 10 ins.
Two groups of 3 bright lights in the skies above South Wales. ;
1 set over the Cardiff Area: I red, 1 blue and 1 yellow arranged in a straight line, very
1 set over the Pontypridd area: all white very bright and arranged in a triangle.
C. Exgct position of observer.
Outside his house in the Rhonda Valley, exact address below.
D, How observed.
With the naked eye.
1 set over Cardiff, the other set over Pontypridd, compass angles not known.
F. Angle of sight.
Approx 20 degrees above the skyline.
G. Distance.
Cardiff set approx 10 - 15 miles.
Pontypridd set approx 4 miles.
BH. Movements.
Cardiff set:
Alllights stationary for a few minutes then middle light shot off at great speed. Other two
bghts faded away. After a further few minutes all 3 returned and gradually faded over a
couple of minutes.
Pontypridd set:
Stationary at all times, sky clouded over and when cleared lights were gone.
1 ological conditions duri ations.
Dry, clear with patchy cloud.

ADGE1

UFO REPORTS - 15 & 22 FEB 99

You asked me to research these UFO reports to see if there were any AD involvement, interest or
corroboration of sighting.

AD ACTIVITY

Have checked AD activity for the periods concerned. the results are as follows;

15 Feb 99 (Period 1015-104S5hrs).

UKCAOC.  Norecord of AD activity over the duration of the UFO report. Particularly,

"there was no QRA(I) activity, no routine UK AD flying and no exercises in progress between

those times.

RAF Neatishead. RAF Neatishead were not involved in any AD, exercise or air activity
during the period of interest.

RAF Buchan. RAF Buchan were not involved in any AD, exercise or air activity
during the period of interest.

Radar Investigations. Due to the source of this report and the description of the observed
traffic, further investigation was carried out by examination of recorded radar data from the
following sources:

-- RAF Buchan Reference Telecon SERMON 220915ZMar99 ~ RAF Buchan
radar information examined for the time and area of the reported sighting by Buchan
ops staff. No traffic matching the report was seen. VHS video tape en-route to
HQ11/18Gp, radar tape impounded.

-- LATCC West Drayton Reference Telecon Section 40 PAA byavered) -
LATCC radar information examined for the time and area of the reported sighting by
AIS staff. No traffic matching the report was seen. VHS video tape en-route to
HQ11/18Gp, radar tape impounded.

-- SCATCC Prestwick Reference Letter PWK/20/1/ATCC dated 15 Mar 99 —
SCATCC radar information examined for the time and area of the reported sighting by
SO2 ASACS Ops1. No traffic matching the report was seen. VHS video tape with
HQ11/18Gp, radar tape impounded.

CONCLUSION

There was no AD activity at the time of the report.

No exercises involving RAF AD units were in progress at the time.

UNC LASSIAIER,

The National Archives' reference DEFE 24/2008/1

UNCLASSIFIED

Recorded radar data replays do not support the sighting.

Report of no further interest to HQAD.

22 Feb 99 (Period 2030-2100hrs).

- UKCAOC. No record of AD activity over the duration of the UFO report. Particularly,
there was no QRA(]) activity and no exercises in- progress between those times.

- RAF Neatishead. RAF Neatishead were controlling 4 x Leeming F3s (SAMSON 1-4 -11
Sqn) in area 7 (Lincolnshire MTRA) at the time of the reported sighting. These ac are
discounted from contributing to the sighting for the following reasons;

-- The Westerly boundary of Area 7 is over 35nm East of Littleover.

--- Any traffic inadvertently straying West towards Littleover from Area 7 would
infringe the E. Midlands Airport CTZ, which would be a serious matter and
subject to a special report.

-- The F3 ac were not stationary, and
-- They were not dropping flares.

--- Notwithstanding the above, F3s do not show steady lights when carrying out
Practice Intercepts, only flashing anti-collision strobes.

CONCLUSION

- There was no QRA(I) activity at the time of the report.

- No exercises involving RAF AD units were in progress at the time.

- There was AD training flying in the Lincolnshire MTRA, but this could not have produced the
sighting observed.

- Report of no further interest to HQAD.

NOTE

- I have impounded 3 radar tapes upon your authority for the purpose of examining recorded
radar data for evidence of the 15 Feb 99 sighting. If you agree, I will release each radar tape
once I have received the VHS video of the area/time of interest. Please advise.

Sqn Ldr
SO2 ASACS Ops1

. 6-18" MANUAL OF AIR TRAFFIC SERVICES Part 1
@ Chapter 4
® 3 UNIDENTIFIED FLYING OBJECTS

A controller receiving a report about an unidentified flying object must obtain as much as
possible of the information required to complete a report in the format shown below,

Report of Unidentified Flying Object

A Date, Time and Duration of Sighting
Local times to be quoted.

B Description of Object
Number of objects, size, shape, colours, brightness, sound, smell, etc.

«Exact Position of Observer
Geographical location, indoors or outdoors, stationary or moving.

DO How Observed
Naked eye, binoculars, other optical device, still or movie camera.

E Direction in which Object was First Seen
A landmark may be more useful than a badly estimared bearing.

F- Angular Elevation of Object
Estimated heights are'‘unreliable.

G Distance of Object from Observer
By reference to a known landmark wherever possible.

H Movements of Object
Changes in E, F and G may be of more use than estimates of course and speed,

J Meteorological Conditions During Obscrvations
Moving clouds, haze, mist, etc.

K Nearby Objects
Telephone or high-voltage lines; reservoir, lake or dam; swamp or marsh; river; high
buildings, tall chimneys, steeples, spires, TV or radio masts; airfields, generating
plant; factories; pits or other sites with floodlights or other lighting.

L To Whom Reported
Police, military organisations, the press, etc.

M Name and Address of Informant

N Any Background Information on the Informant that may be Volunteered

QO Other Witnesses

P Date and Time of Receipt of Report / é
The details are to be telephoned immediately to AIS (Military), LATCC. GQ Ss

The completed report is to be sent by the Originating air traffic service unit to the
Ministry of Defence Sec (AS),

A LIST OF TELEPHONE NUMBERS AND LOCATIONS IS SHOWN IN THE

UFO REPORTS FROM CREDIBLE WITNESSES

1. In accordance with current procedure, I would be grateful if you would determine whether
any unusual air activity of air defence significance was associated with the 2 attached UFO

reports.

2. The report from the Prestwick SATCO is unusual and may be the subject of subsequent
publicity. I would be grateful, therefore, if this incident could be thoroughly scrutinised to
determine whether a similar phenomenon was seen on sensors available to ASACS. Additionally,
please determine whether a radar recording of the incident has been retained by Prestwick and, if
SO, request that it be kept for future reference.

3. A reply by 26 Mar 99 would be appreciated.
